Tariff, Part VIII, Subpart N
COST RECOVERY PROTECTION AND AGREEMENT
Page 20

PDF page 69

Tariff, Part VIII, Subpart N, section 441
Cost Recovery Protection for Firm Transmission Service
(a) Cost Recovery Assurances: With respect to Eligible Customers that intend to request any 
form of Firm Transmission Service from Transmission Provider on behalf of an Eligible Load or 
Large Load, a Transmission Owner shall either:
(i)
Enter into a Cost Recovery Agreement in the form found in Tariff, Part IX, 
Subpart P with the applicable Eligible Customer and Eligible Load or Large Load.  The 
Transmission Owner counterparty shall be the Transmission Owner that owns the transmission 
facilities with which the Eligible Load or Large Load will interconnect.  In the case of an 
Eligible Load or Large Load that will not directly interconnect with a Transmission Owner, the 
Transmission Owner counterparty shall be the Transmission Owner to which the applicable 
Eligible Customer is connected and from whom the Eligible Customer intends to take Firm 
Transmission Service.  The purpose of the Cost Recovery Agreement is to ensure the Eligible 
Customer takes a minimum level of Firm Transmission Service on behalf of the Eligible Load or 
Large Load based on the committed load level and the applicable rate for the Firm Transmission 
Service, or, if not, the Eligible Load or Large Load shall be required to make a shortfall payment 
representing any difference between the committed minimum level of service and the actual 
level of service taken.
(ii)
Enter into, subject to FERC acceptance, a separate agreement between at least the 
Transmission Owner and an Eligible Load or Large Load to prevent cost shifting among 
transmission customers.  Such separate agreement will be determined to adequately prevent cost 
shifts if such agreement, in conjunction with relevant federal and state regulatory requirements, 
including applicable retail service tariffs, results in minimum Firm Transmission Service and 
credit standards, or other comparable protections.
An Eligible Customer and/or Eligible Load or Large Load will be required to enter into and 
execute a Cost Recovery Agreement or an agreement that meets the standards set forth in 
subsection (a)(ii) in order to proceed past the Soft Close Date associated with the applicable PJM 
Fast Energization Cycle, and obtain any form of Firm Transmission Service from the 
Transmission Provider.
(b) Preservation of Legacy Treatment: No Cost Recovery Agreement or separate agreement 
under subsection (a) above shall be required with respect to Eligible Load or Large Loads 
covered by existing agreements that meet the criteria set forth in subsection (a)(ii) that have 
already been filed and are pending acceptance, or that have been accepted by FERC.  Any 
agreement entered into prior to the effective date of this subsection not filed with FERC will not 
be required to be filed and accepted by FERC; provided, however, in any complaint filed 
pursuant to Section 206 of the Federal Power Act in which it is claimed that such agreement is 
not just and reasonable or is unduly discriminatory within the meaning of the Federal Power Act, 
such agreement shall not be determined to be unjust and unreasonable or unduly discriminatory 
if it meets the standard set forth in subsection (a)(ii).
(c) Tender and Negotiation of Cost Recovery Agreement:  As applicable, consistent with 
subsections (a) and (b) above, Transmission Owner shall tender a draft of the Cost Recovery 
Page 21

PDF page 70

Agreement to Eligible Customer and Eligible Load or Large Load within 30 days of the 
completion of the Transmission Owner’s retail load interconnection study.  
Upon receipt of the draft Cost Recovery Agreement, Eligible Customer, as well as the Eligible 
Load or Large Load, shall provide the Transmission Owner with any written comments on the 
draft Cost Recovery Agreement within seven (7) days. The parties will conduct further 
negotiations as required, with such negotiations to be completed within twenty-five (25) days of 
the Transmission Owner’s tender of the draft Cost Recovery Agreement.  
(d) Execution and Filing of Cost Recovery Agreement: Not later than ten (10) days after the 
end of the negotiation period set forth in subsection (c) above, Eligible Customer and the Eligible 
Load or Large Load shall (i) execute the Cost Recovery Agreement and return it to Transmission 
Owner; and (ii) provide the Credit Support specified in the Cost Recovery Agreement.
If the Eligible Load or Large Load fails to execute the Cost Recovery Agreement or provide the 
required Credit Support in the specified time period, Eligible Customer shall not be permitted to 
proceed through the applicable Fast Energization Cycle.  
The Transmission Owner shall file any executed Cost Recovery Agreement, if required, with 
FERC within twenty-one (21) days of the other parties executing the Cost Recovery Agreement.
(e) Crediting of Termination Amounts: If the effective date of an Early Termination or Partial 
Termination, as defined in a Cost Recovery Agreement, occurs prior to the first Service Date 
specified in such agreement, and the Eligible Load or Large Load that is a party to that 
agreement is required to make a termination payment, as applicable, such termination payment 
or other amounts received pursuant to such obligation, including any security drawn on by 
Transmission Owner, shall be divided amongst the pool of Transmission Owners constructing
facilities needed to provide the related transmission service, but which will no longer be 
constructed, including the Transmission Owner that is a party to the Cost Recovery Agreement, 
as follows:
(i)
Within thirty (30) days of such termination, the Transmission Owner that is party 
to the Cost Recovery Agreement (referred to herein as the “contracting Transmission Owner”) 
shall notify all other Transmission Owners that have been designated to construct and own 
Interconnection Reliability Reinforcements necessary to provide Firm Transmission Service to 
an Eligible Customer on behalf of the terminating Eligible Load or Large Load (referred to 
herein as the “third party Transmission Owners”) of such termination.  For purposes of this 
subsection, third party Transmission Owners shall include all Transmission Owners designated 
to construct and own Interconnection Reliability Reinforcements identified in the PJM Fast 
Energization Cycle in which the terminating Eligible Load or Large Load was studied, and for 
which the contracting Transmission Owner’s Zone received a cost responsibility share.
(ii)
Each third-party Transmission Owner shall, within thirty (30) days of the deadline 
for filings its FERC Form 1 that includes all Eligible Costs, provide an account of such Eligible 
Costs to the contracting Transmission Owner.  For purposes of this subsection “Eligible Costs” 
consist of all expenditures, costs, and cancellation fees incurred or due to commitments made by 
the third-party Transmission Owner to engineer, design, procure, construct, own, operate, 
Page 22

PDF page 71

maintain, and service the Interconnection Reliability Reinforcements needed to serve the Eligible 
Customer taking transmission service on behalf of the Eligible Load or Large Load, but which 
will no longer be constructed, which (a) are incurred through the end of the calendar year in 
which notice of termination was received from the contracting Transmission Owner, and (b) are 
included in amounts reported in the Transmission Owner’s FERC Form 1 covering that calendar 
year.  
Any failure by a third-party Transmission Owner to provide such information within the 
timeframe above shall bar said Transmission Owner from receiving a portion of amounts paid by 
Eligible Load or Large Load as set forth below.
(iii)
Within thirty (30) days of (a) receiving all Transmission Owner accountings in 
accordance with subsection (ii) above, and (b) receipt of termination amounts from the Eligible 
Load or Large Load that is party to the Cost Recovery Agreement,  the contracting Transmission 
Owner shall divide such termination amounts among itself and the pool of third-party 
Transmission Owners based on each Transmission Owner’s pro rata share of the total amount of 
applicable expenditures, costs and cancellation fees, and disburse such amounts to the third-party 
Transmission Owners accordingly.  
For purposes of determining these pro rata shares, the contracting Transmission Owner shall be 
permitted to include Eligible Costs associated not only with its applicable Interconnection 
Reliability Reinforcements but also any other transmission facilities, including Supplemental 
Projects and Direct Connection Facilities, that it was constructing in order to provide 
interconnection or transmission service to the Eligible Load or Large Load, or Eligible Customer 
taking transmission service on behalf of the Eligible Load or Large Load.
To the extent that additional termination funds are received or collected from the Eligible Load 
or Large Load after an initial distribution is made under this subsection, contracting 
Transmission Owner will make additional disbursements using the same methodology within 
fifteen (15) days of receipt of such amounts.
(iv)
Nothing in this subsection (e) is intended or shall be interpreted to prevent any 
Transmission Owner from seeking cost recovery through an abandoned plant recovery filing 
made with FERC or through any other proceeding, except insofar as the Transmission Owner has 
received amounts pursuant to the process set forth in this subsection (e), it shall be prohibited 
from requesting recovery of such amounts in any future proceeding.
(v)
To the extent a Transmission Owner elects to satisfy its obligation under 
subsection (a) above through a separate agreement as described in subsection (a)(ii), it shall 
include in such separate agreement reasonably comparable termination payment crediting 
provisions as set forth in this subsection (e), as determined by FERC.
(vi)
Any disputes under this subsection (e) shall be subject to the dispute resolution 
procedure set forth in Schedule 5 of the Operating Agreement.

COST RECOVERY AGREEMENT
This Cost Recovery Agreement (“Agreement” or “CRA”) is made and entered into as of 
[Insert Date] by and among [Transmission Owner], a [State and Entity Type] (“Transmission 
Owner”) and [Large Load Customer], a [State and Entity Type] (“Large Load Customer”), and
[Eligible Customer], a [State and Entity Type] (“Eligible Customer”). Transmission Owner, 
Eligible Customer, and Large Load Customer are referred to individually as a “Party” and 
collectively as the “Parties.” 
Large Load Customer will be responsible for all payment and Credit Support obligations 
assigned to Large Load Customer under this CRA. Eligible Customer will remain responsible for 
the transmission-service, information-sharing, and administrative obligations expressly assigned 
to Eligible Customer under this CRA.
RECITALS
WHEREAS, Large Load Customer intends to construct, maintain, and operate new or 
modified facilities located at or near [Facility Address] that will constitute a Large Load (the 
“Large Load Facilities”), with an anticipated initial service date of [Date] (the “Commercial 
Operation Date”);
WHEREAS, Large Load Customer has completed retail interconnection studies 
conducted by Transmission Owner and/or one or more of its affiliates;
WHEREAS,
following completion of the applicable Transmission Owner’s 
interconnection study process, the Parties are entering into this CRA based on the Initial Load 
Commitment, proposed load ramp, and anticipated Service Dates identified in Exhibit A;
WHEREAS, Eligible Customer intends to request Network Integration Transmission 
Service (“NITS”), Firm Point-to-Point Transmission Service, or Firm Contract Demand 
Transmission Service (“FCDS”) under the PJM Open Access Transmission Tariff (“PJM Tariff”) 
on behalf of the Large Load Customer to serve the Large Load Facilities , each of which 
constitutes “Firm Transmission Service” under this CRA;
WHEREAS, Exhibit A identifies the initial amount and type of Firm Transmission 
Service to be requested by Eligible Customer on behalf of Large Load Customer, together with 
the load ramp and anticipated Service Dates;
WHEREAS, following completion of the applicable application and study process to 
obtain the Firm Transmission Service, Exhibit A and the related calculations under this CRA will 
be updated to reflect the type, amount, and timing of Firm Transmission Service established in 
the Final PJM Agreement;
WHEREAS, in reliance on the Firm Load Commitment and in accordance with the PJM 
Tariff and other applicable agreements, the Transmission Owner intends to construct, own, 
Page 26

PDF page 75

operate, maintain, service, and recover the costs associated with transmission facilities necessary 
to serve the Large Load Facilities;
WHEREAS, this CRA establishes the payment and Credit Support obligations described 
herein and allocates the financial risk arising if Firm Transmission Service-related revenues 
attributable to service actually taken for the Large Load Facilities are less than the Committed 
Revenue Contribution.
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the premises and the mutual covenants and 
undertakings set forth herein, the receipt and sufficiency of which are acknowledged, the Parties, 
intending to be legally bound, agree as follows:
ARTICLE 1
DEFINITIONS
1.0
Defined Terms 
Capitalized terms used in this CRA have the meanings set forth below. Capitalized terms used 
but not defined in this CRA have the meanings set forth in the PJM Tariff.
(a)
“Actual Revenue Contribution” means, for each Measurement Period, the Firm 
Transmission Service-related revenues attributable to Firm Transmission Service actually 
taken by 
Eligible Customer to serve the Large Load Facilities, as calculated in 
accordance with Section 5.2 and Exhibit B.
(b) 
“Agreement” or “CRA” means this Cost Recovery Agreement, including its Exhibits and 
any amendments that become effective in accordance with this CRA and applicable law.
(c)
“Applicable Credits” means the amounts identified in Article 9 and Exhibit D, Section 4, 
that are deducted in calculating the Early Termination Amount or Partial Termination 
Amount, without duplication.
(d)
“Applicable Firm Transmission Service Rate” means, for each Measurement Period, the 
Transmission Owner’s rate or rates then in effect under the PJM Tariff for the applicable 
Firm Transmission Service in the applicable Transmission Zone, as used to calculate the 
charges corresponding to the Firm Load Commitment.
(e)
“Applicable Termination Percentage” means the percentage set forth in Exhibit D for the 
period in which the termination or Partial Termination becomes effective.
(f)
“Business Day” means any day other than a Saturday, Sunday, or holiday recognized by 
the North American Electric Reliability Corporation.
(g)
“Commercial Operation Date” means the anticipated initial service date for the Large 
Load Facilities identified in Exhibit A.
(h)
“Committed Revenue Contribution”
means, for each Measurement Period, the 
contractually required portion of the Full Firm Transmission Service Benchmark, 
calculated by applying the Minimum Commitment Percentage in accordance with Section 
5.1 and Exhibit B.
(i)

provide and maintain under Section 6.1 to secure Large Load Customer’s payment and 
other financial obligations under this CRA.

Service Benchmarks for all Measurement Periods remaining in the Term, including any 
portion of the applicable Measurement Period that remains incomplete. Before the 
recalculation required under Section 3.2, the Credit Support Requirement shall be the 
Initial Credit Support Requirement.
(k)
“Effective Date” means the date on which this CRA becomes effective as set forth in

(l)
“Early Termination Amount” means the amount payable by Large Load Customer upon 
a termination of this CRA for which an Early Termination Amount is required under

(m)
“Early Termination Offset Amount” means the Firm Transmission Service-related 
revenues received by Transmission Owner during the period corresponding to the 
remaining fixed period from incremental replacement load served at the same Point of 
Delivery, to the extent those revenues correspond to the revenue exposure included in the 
Early Termination Amount or Partial Termination Amount, as determined in accordance 
with Section 9.9.
(n)
“Eligible Customer” means the Person identified as  Eligible Customer in the preamble to 
this CRA, which is an Eligible Customer under the PJM Tariff and the entity responsible 
for taking Firm Transmission Service on behalf of Large Load Customer to serve the 
Large Load Facilities. Eligible Customer is responsible only for the obligations expressly 
assigned to Eligible Customer under this CRA and is not responsible for Large Load 
Customer’s payment or Credit Support obligations.
(o)

(p)

agreement executed under the PJM Tariff that identifies the Firm Transmission Service to 
be taken by Eligible Customer on behalf of Large Load Customer.
(q)
“Firm Load Commitment” means the amount of firm load identified in Exhibit A, 
together with the corresponding level of Firm Transmission Service that Eligible 
Customer has committed to take on behalf of  Large Load Customer and any associated 
load-ramp milestones and Service Dates.
(r)

(x)
“Large Load” shall mean end-use customer(s) load that has a cumulative peak load of 50 
megawatts or greater at a single electrical site behind one or more points of

multiple points of interconnection with affiliated load facilities behind them, all affiliated

(aa)
