Id. at Articles 3.1-3.2 and Exhibit A.

PDF page 316

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 34 of 43 
Committed Revenue Contribution for the applicable Measurement Period, the Large Load 
1 
Customer is required to pay only the difference, or Shortfall Amount.  Credit Support 
2 
secures the Large Load Customer’s payment and other financial obligations under the 
3 
CRA, including any Shortfall Amount, Early Termination Amount, or Partial Termination 
4 
Amount.48  The CRA’s early and partial termination provisions likewise protect against the 
5 
applicable portion of the remaining projected revenue commitment if the transmission 
6 
request or commitment is withdrawn, the Large Load is abandoned or terminated, or its 
7 
capacity is permanently reduced.  In the event of termination before the first Service Date, 
8 
the Transmission Owner may draw on Credit Support to satisfy the applicable termination 
9 
payment.  That payment is applied first to Project Development Costs associated with 
10 
transmission facilities that will no longer be needed to serve the Large Load, including 
11 
such costs incurred by other Transmission Owners, thereby ensuring that these costs will 
12 
not be included in transmission rate base and recovered from other customers.49
13 
Q. 
Please explain how the proposed CRA is designed to address the risk of cost shifts 
14 
among wholesale transmission customers. 
15 
A. 
[Hammer] The PJM Transmission Owners’ proposed CRA combines a minimum revenue 
16 
contribution, financial-security requirements, and flexibility to tailor specified terms to the 
17 
applicable Transmission Owner and customer parties.  That framework is intended to 
18 
provide necessary wholesale customer protections while promoting timely Large Load 
19 
integration.  Each of the CRA’s financial security requirement addresses a different aspect 
20 
48
Id. at Articles 5.1-5.5, 6.1–6.5, 7.6 and Exhibits B–C. 
49
Proposed Tariff, Part VIII, Subpart N, section 441(e); Proposed pro forma CRA, Part IX, Subpart P at 
Articles 9.1-9.12 and Exhibit D.

PDF page 317

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 35 of 43 
of wholesale transmission cost shift risk.  The minimum revenue commitment addresses 
1 
the risk that anticipated Firm Transmission Service revenues associated with serving a 
2 
Large Load do not materialize.  Credit Support provides financial assurance that the Large 
3 
Load Customer can satisfy its transmission payment obligations over the remaining term 
4 
of the CRA.  Finally, early and partial termination amounts protect against the loss of the 
5 
anticipated revenue contribution if the Large Load Customer terminates or permanently 
6

7 
provide reasonable assurance that the financial risks associated with the Large Load 
8 
Customer’s Firm Transmission Service commitment are not shifted to other wholesale 
9 
transmission customers.50
10 
Q.
Please explain the minimum revenue contribution mechanism and how it protects 
11 
other wholesale transmission customers from potential cost shifts. 
12 
A. 
[Hammer] The minimum revenue contribution mechanism establishes a minimum level 
13 
of transmission revenue that the Large Load Customer is responsible for providing, through 
14 
transmission service payments made by an Eligible Customer on its behalf, during each 
15 
Measurement Period, generally one year.  The CRA establishes a Committed Revenue 
16 
Contribution based on the projected Firm Transmission Service charges associated with 
17 
the applicable load commitment, multiplied by the applicable Minimum Commitment 
18 
Percentage, which ranges from 80 to 100 percent.  For each Measurement Period, the 
19 
Transmission Owner then compares the customer’s actual contribution to the Transmission 
20 
Owner’s revenue requirement, based on its actual use, to its Committed Revenue 
21 
50
Proposed Tariff, Part VIII, Subpart N, section 441(a); Proposed pro forma CRA, Part IX, Subpart P at Articles 
5-6, 9.

PDF page 318

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 36 of 43 
Contribution.  If actual revenues fall below the committed amount, the Large Load 
1 
Customer must pay the difference as a Shortfall Amount.  The minimum revenue 
2 
contribution is initially determined at CRA execution prior to the Large Load customer 
3 
entering the PJM Fast Energization Process based on the amount of service to be requested 
4 
on behalf of the Large Load (the “Initial Load Commitment”), and then updated at the end 
5 
of the PJM Fast Energization Process based on any changes made during that process (the 
6 
“Firm Load Commitment”).51
7 
This mechanism mitigates potential cost shifts by ensuring that the Large Load 
8 
Customer provides a minimum level of revenue associated with its Firm Transmission 
9 
Service commitment, even if its load does not materialize or ramp as expected.  At the 
10 
same time, revenues attributable to serving the Large Load are counted against that 
11 
commitment, so the Shortfall Amount reflects only the difference needed to satisfy the 
12 
agreed minimum contribution.  Shortfall Amounts are credited against the Transmission 
13 
Owner’s transmission revenue requirement, thereby reducing the costs that otherwise could 
14 
be recovered from other wholesale transmission customers.52
15 
Q.
Why is the proposed Minimum Commitment Percentage range of 80 to 100 percent 
16 
reasonable? 
17 
A. 
[Hammer] In my opinion, the proposed range is reasonable because it establishes a 
18 
meaningful minimum revenue commitment while allowing individual Transmission 
19 
Owners to require a higher commitment where appropriate based on their particular 
20 
51
Proposed pro forma CRA, Part IX, Subpart P at Articles 3.1-3.2, 5.1-5.5 and Exhibits A-B.

Id. at Article 7.6.

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 37 of 43 
circumstances.  The 80 percent floor is also consistent with prior agreements accepted by 
1 
the Commission.53
2 
Q.
How is the Credit Support Requirement determined? 
3 
A. 
[Hammer] The Credit Support Requirement is designed to secure the Transmission 
4 
Owner’s projected Firm Transmission Service revenue exposure associated with the Large 
5 
Load Customer’s commitment over the remaining term of the CRA.  It equals the aggregate 
6 
Projected Full Firm Transmission Service Benchmarks for the Measurement Periods 
7 
remaining in the Term, including any incomplete portion of the applicable Measurement 
8 
Period.  Initially, it is based on 100 percent of the projected Firm Transmission Service-
9 
related charges associated with the Initial Load Commitment over the CRA term.  
10 
Following completion of the PJM Fast Energization Process and execution of the relevant 
11 
service agreement with PJM (referred to in the filing as the “Final PJM Agreement”), that 
12 
amount is recalculated based on the Firm Load Commitment, Service Dates, and other 
13 
information established following the Final PJM Agreement.  The Credit Support 
14 
Requirement is then recalculated after each Measurement Period.  Once the Large Load 
15 
Customer has satisfied its obligations for a Measurement Period, that period is removed 
16 
from the calculation.  As a result, the Credit Support Requirement generally declines as 
17 
completed Measurement Periods are reconciled, paid, and removed from the calculation, 
18 
although it may also be adjusted when other relevant inputs change.54
19 
20 
53

Q.
How is the Projected Full Firm Transmission Service Benchmark determined?
1 
A. 
[Hammer] The Projected Full Firm Transmission Service Benchmark represents the 
2 
projected charges for 100 percent of the Large Load Customer’s applicable Firm 
3 
Transmission Service for each Measurement Period.  For each Measurement Period 
4 
included in the Credit Support Requirement, the Projected Full Firm Transmission Service 
5 
Benchmark equals the Firm Transmission Service-related charges that would apply if 
6 
Eligible Customer took Firm Transmission Service corresponding to the Initial Load 
7 
Commitment or Firm Load Commitment, as applicable, scheduled to apply during that 
8 
Measurement Period.  It is calculated using the customer’s Firm Load Commitment, the 
9 
applicable Firm Transmission Service rates, and the portion of the Measurement Period 
10 
during which those load levels and rates apply.  The calculation also reflects any rate 
11 
change accepted by the Commission and scheduled to become effective during the 
12 
applicable Measurement Period.55
13 
Q. 
Why is the Credit Support Requirement adjusted over the life of the CRA, and why 
14 
is that approach reasonable? 
15 
A. 
[Hammer] The Credit Support Requirement is adjusted over the life of the CRA so that 
16 
the amount of financial security continues to reflect the Transmission Owner’s full 
17 
projected Firm Transmission Service revenue exposure over the remaining term of the 
18 
CRA.  After each Measurement Period is finally reconciled and the Large Load Customer 
19 
has paid all amounts due for that period, that Measurement Period is removed from the 
20 
Credit Support Requirement.  The requirement is also recalculated when relevant inputs 
21 
55
Id. at Exhibit C, Sections 1-3.

PDF page 321

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 39 of 43 
change, such as the Firm Load Commitment, Service Dates, or applicable Firm 
1 
Transmission Service rates.56
2 
This approach is reasonable because it appropriately aligns the amount of financial 
3 
security with the exposure that remains under the CRA.  The Transmission Owner, to the 
4 
benefit of other customers, retains financial assurance for its full projected exposure over 
5 
the remaining term, while the Large Load Customer receives the benefit of a reduction in 
6 
Credit Support as it satisfies its obligations over time.  Once a Measurement Period has 
7 
been completed, reconciled, and paid, there is no longer a need to include that period in the 
8 
amount being secured.  The result is a Credit Support Requirement that remains sufficient 
9 
to protect against prospective revenue risk without requiring the Large Load Customer to 
10 
collateralize exposure that has already been eliminated through performance.  
11 
Q.
What are the early and partial termination provisions?
12 
A. 
[Hammer] The early and partial termination provisions address circumstances in which 
13 
the Firm Transmission Service request is: (i) withdrawn or abandoned, (ii) the CRA 
14 
terminates before the end of its agreed term, (iii) the project is abandoned or ceases 
15 
operations, or (iv) the Large Load Customer permanently reduces its Firm Load 
16 
Commitment.  In those circumstances, the Large Load Customer is responsible for an Early 
17 
Termination Amount or Partial Termination Amount.  The Early Termination Amount 
18 
protects a specified portion of the revenue contribution projected for the remainder of the 
19 
CRA and is generally based on the applicable Termination Percentage and the present value 
20 
of the remaining Projected Committed Revenue Contributions, less applicable credits.  For 
21 
56
Id. at Article 6.5 and Exhibit C, Sections 1-4.

PDF page 322

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 40 of 43 
a partial termination, the amount is adjusted to reflect the permanent reduction in the Large 
1 
Load Customer’s Firm Load Commitment.57
2 
Q.
Why are these termination provisions necessary?
3 
A.  
[Hammer] These termination provisions are necessary because the Transmission Owner 
4 
may make long-term transmission planning and investment decisions based on the Large 
5 
Load Customer’s Firm Load Commitment.  If the customer terminates or materially 
6 
reduces that commitment, the associated transmission revenues may no longer materialize. 
7 
The termination provisions mitigate that risk by assigning an appropriate portion of the 
8 
remaining revenue exposure to the Large Load Customer whose project and Firm Load 
9 
Commitment gave rise to the exposure, thereby reducing the potential for the resulting 
10 
revenue shortfall to be shifted to other wholesale transmission customers. 
11 
Q.
How are the Early Termination Amount and Partial Termination Amount 
12 
determined? 
13 
A. 
[Hammer] The Early Termination Amount is generally calculated by applying the 
14 
applicable Termination Percentage to the present value of the remaining Projected 
15 
Committed Revenue Contributions, less any applicable credits. The Termination 
16 
Percentage increases as the project progresses, from 25 percent before execution of the 
17 
Final PJM Agreement, to 50 percent during the first 180 days following execution of the 
18 
Final PJM Agreement, to 75 percent thereafter and before the first Service Date, and to 100 
19 
percent on and after the first Service Date.58  The present value calculation reflects the 
20 
57
Id. at Articles 9.1-9.9 and Exhibit D. 
58
Id. at Exhibit D, Section 1.

PDF page 323

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 41 of 43 
Large Load Customer’s remaining Firm Load Commitment, the Minimum Commitment 
1 
Percentage, applicable transmission service rates, rate components and billing 
2 
determinants identified in Exhibit B to the CRA, and the Transmission Owner’s then-
3 
effective FERC-approved Weighted Average Cost of Capital as the discount rate.  
4 
The Partial Termination Amount applies the same framework to the portion of the 
5 
Firm Load Commitment that is permanently reduced. Generally, it is determined by 
6 
multiplying the Early Termination Amount by the percentage of committed MW being 
7 
reduced.59
8 
Q.
How are the Termination Percentages determined and why are they reasonable?
9 
A. 
[Hammer] The Termination Percentages increase as the project progresses, reflecting the 
10 
increasing financial exposure associated with the Large Load Customer’s commitment.  
11 
Under the CRA, the applicable percentage increases from 25 percent prior to execution of 
12 
the Final PJM Agreement to 100 percent on and after the first Service Date.60
13 
This approach is reasonable because it aligns the customer’s termination 
14 
responsibility with the stage of its commitment and the Transmission Owner’s increasing 
15 
exposure as the project advances.  At earlier stages, when the project is less advanced and 
16 
the Transmission Owner’s exposure is correspondingly lower, only a portion of the 
17 
remaining committed revenue exposure is assigned to the Large Load Customer.  As the 
18 
project advances and the Transmission Owner increasingly relies on the Firm Load 
19 
Commitment for planning, investment, and anticipated revenue recovery, the Large Load 
20 
59
Id. at Articles 9.7-9.9 and Exhibit D, Sections 1-5. 
60
Id. at Exhibit D, Section 1.

PDF page 324

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 42 of 43 
Customer assumes a greater share of that exposure.  Once service begins, the Large Load 
1 
Customer is responsible for 100 percent of its remaining committed revenue obligation in 
2 
the event of termination.  
3 
Q.
Why is the Termination Amount based on the minimum revenue obligation rather 
4 
than projected full transmission revenues, as used to calculate the Credit Support 
5 
Requirement? 
6 
A. 
[Hammer] The Termination Amount represents an actual payment obligation, whereas the 
7 
Credit Support Requirement represents financial security for potential exposure.  Because 
8 
the Large Load Customer’s contractual minimum revenue obligation is determined by 
9 
applying the Minimum Commitment Percentage to projected transmission revenues, it is 
10 
reasonable for the Termination Amount to be based on that committed revenue obligation 
11 
rather than 100 percent of projected revenues.  This distinction ensures that the more 
12 
conservative Credit Support Requirement provides financial assurance against potential 
13 
exposure without converting that higher level of security into a greater payment obligation 
14 
upon termination. 
15 
Thus, the two provisions serve different but complementary purposes: Credit 
16 
Support secures the Transmission Owner’s full projected exposure, while the Termination 
17 
Amount reflects the Large Load Customer’s contractual payment responsibility upon 
18 
termination.61
19 
61
Id. at Exhibit C, Sections 1-3, 7 and Exhibit D, Sections 2-3.

PDF page 325

Exhibit No. PTO-0001 
Page 43 of 43 
Q.
In your opinion, does the proposed CRA provide an appropriate framework for 
1 
mitigating potential cost shifts associated with Large Loads among wholesale 
2 
transmission customers? 
3 
A. 
[Hammer] Yes. The proposed CRA provides an appropriate set of financial protections 
4 
designed to mitigate the risk that costs associated with serving a Large Load will be shifted 
5 
to other wholesale transmission customers.  It achieves this through a set of  interlocking 
6 
mechanisms. 
7 
The proposed CRA establishes a minimum revenue commitment to provide a 
8 
defined level of transmission revenue even if the Large Load does not materialize or ramp 
9 
as expected. It requires Credit Support based on 100 percent of projected Firm 
10 
Transmission Service revenues, providing a strong financial backstop for the Transmission 
11 
Owner’s potential exposure.  And, if the customer terminates or reduces its commitment, 
12 
the early and partial termination provisions require the customer to bear an appropriate 
13 
portion of its remaining committed revenue obligation.62
14 
Collectively, these provisions appropriately allocate the financial risks associated 
15 
with the Large Load Customer’s Firm Transmission Service commitment to that customer, 
16 
while calibrating the customer’s payment and Credit Support obligations to the nature and 
17 
extent of the exposure being addressed. 
18 
Q. 
Does this complete your testimony at this time? 
19 
A. 
[Blazunas and Hammer] Yes.
20 
62
Id. at Articles 5-6, 9 and Exhibits B-D.

Mr. Blazunas specializes in regulatory strategy and rate case expertise for regulated electric 
and gas utility rate proceedings. He joined Concentric in 2021 after nine years in progressively 
senior roles within the regulatory departments of large investor-owned electric utilities. Most 
recently, at Exelon Corp., he managed a team overseeing cost of service, revenue allocation,
